THE EXANIINEIL FRIDAY. ArtIUST 1301.1887.

The decea,el, who leaves • widow and large family to mourn his losousucceeded Ihis father the Into NI, William Jonra no senior 'partner in the firm oh William Jones and Sons, 8... The firm which was established in 1840 ' among other improvement. in the Indus- try were the inventors and Nest manilla°. • tuners of the vertical tubular system to steam boilers now in almost universal aid Mr. Thomats Jones retired from businsas ab mt 12 years , ago.and shortly afterwards the firm cessed to exist. Mr. Jones hie filled many important public officer. Be represented the old Nortli•West Ward in the Town Council continuously from November, 1863, to November, 1878, at which latter date he did not seek re-election owing to ill-health. During this period he did good and valued work on then..., Market, and Cemetery Committees. Mr, Jones was elected an oven«an of the poor in 1867,and for some yea. an active member of the Board of Ourdians. He was also one of the founders of the LW Peoples' Treat, tel haa been vies-president of the committee since its formation. lie was the oldeet .arriving member of the Masonic Lodge of Light& In politics he was a Liberal, and he and hie family have been for many yeara prominent members of the Wesleyan body. 'LAU& FOIL DAILLOE TO A BICYCLE

At Ilarrington County Court on Thursday.before his is onour Judge Plouikee Arthur Frost, of High. street, Newtonhl..-Wlllows,clairned .Cl 3 from Thomas it. Garton. Bouthworth, Newton-le-Willows, for damage done to • bicycle and for the non-return of such bicycle when demanded. Mr. Marshall, bar- rister (instructed by Mr. Stuart) appeared for the plaintiff, and Mr. A. Browne defended. In his open• inn statement, Mr. Marshall said the plaintiff wee • bookbinder at 3lessrs, MCCorguodale's printing works, Newton. and the defendant was. seed nor- chant. Plaintiff bought the bicycle in question from Mr Mewdaley at Newton In May ltetri the price being S 5 in cash and Mr. Slawdsley allowing , bun .C 6 10 for his old machine, making the pm- really .Cll 10, It had however no tyres on the wheels and these cost an eaten sum of about 30t. Plaintiff did not do much riding on his machine, only making GOO long journey to Di-eaten Castle and back, • di, ance of about 70 miles. On the 27th August when coming hack from • ride he met Garton who borrowed the bicycle for a ride to ' St. Helens the next day. and promised to return it by six o'clock the next evening. As a matter of fact, however, he did not return it till April tbia yearend it was in a very much damaged condition. Mr. Marshall then described the damages which in- cluded nnotner and inferior tyro on one of the wheels, A straining of the down tube and forka and spu ianir ether defects.—Cridence was given by the if and others in support of thin statement. I lite Honour intimated that he would be unable to hear all the witnesses that day.—The parties then had a consultation and it was ultimately agreed I that plaintiff should have the money paid into court, aun base the defendant's costs from the time the money was paid into Court, and each party to pay their own costs prior to the paying of the , money into court. Judgmenc wae gje,. accord. ,
